The authors declare that they have no conflict of interest.

Appendix A
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded tensor product for fermionic systems

In fermionic cases,
the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}
could be easily identified as
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector space, i.e.,

\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}=\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(0)}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(1)},\quad\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}=\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}^{(0)}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}^{(1)},

where
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(0)}
,
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(1)}
are defined as

\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(0)}=\{\psi_{\text{s}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}|\hat{P}_{\text{s}}|\psi_{\text{s}}\rangle=|\psi_{\text{s}}\rangle\},\quad\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(1)}=\{\psi_{\text{s}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}|\hat{P}_{\text{s}}|\psi\rangle_{\text{s}}=-|\psi\rangle_{\text{s}}\},

and
\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}^{(0)}
,
\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}^{(1)}
are similarly defined. Intuitively,
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S},\text{E}}^{(0)}
is the subspace of wavefunctions that has even particle numbers, while
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S},\text{E}}^{(1)}
is the subspace of wavefunctions that has odd particle numbers.
An operator
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
acting on
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}
is said to be of even parity, if
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
maps
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(j)}
to
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(j)}
, for
j=0,1
, and is said to be of odd parity if
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
maps
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{(j)}
to
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}^{((j^{\prime})}
for
j=0,1
, where
j^{\prime}=(j+1)\mod 2
. If
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
is neither even nor odd,
it is said to be of indefinite parity otherwise.
Similarly one can define parity of operators acting on
\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}
.
The fermionic creation and annihilation operators,
\hat{c}_{i},\hat{c}_{i}^{\dagger}\in B(\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}})
,
\hat{a}_{j},\hat{a}_{j}^{\dagger}\in B(\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}})
are of odd parity, while the number operators are of even parity.
Note that in this work, all operators that have been used are of definite parity.

The standard
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded tensor product, which preserves the CAR, is defined as follows
[
Ber13
]
:

Definition A.1

(
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded tensor product)
.

For any
\hat{O}_{\text{e}}
acting on
\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}})
and
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
acting on
\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}
of definitive parity, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded tensor product of
\hat{O}_{\text{e}}
and
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
, denoted as
\hat{O}_{\text{e}}\otimes_{g}\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
, is an operator acting on
\mathcal{H}
(
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}
), defined as follows:

\left(\hat{O}_{\mathrm{e}}\otimes_{g}\hat{O}_{\mathrm{s}}\right)\left(\psi_{\mathrm{e}}\otimes\psi_{\mathrm{s}}\right)=(-1)^{\sigma\left(\hat{O}_{\mathrm{s}}\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\psi_{\mathrm{e}}\right)}\left(\left(\hat{O}_{\mathrm{e}}\psi_{\mathrm{e}}\right)\otimes\left(\hat{O}_{\mathrm{s}}\psi_{\mathrm{s}}\right)\right).

(A.1)

Here
\sigma(\hat{O}_{\text{s}})=0
if
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
is even and
\sigma(\hat{O}_{\text{s}})=1
if
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
is odd, and
\sigma^{\prime}(\psi_{\text{e}})
are defined similarly.

Thus, the generalization of environment and system operators is defined using
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded tensor product with identity operator:

\hat{O}_{\text{e}}|_{\mathcal{H}}=\hat{O}_{\text{e}}\otimes_{g}\hat{1}_{\text{s}},\quad\hat{O}_{\text{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}}=\hat{1}_{\text{e}}\otimes_{g}\hat{O}_{\text{s}}.

Using
Definition

A.1
, we have
\hat{O}_{\text{e}}|_{\mathcal{H}}=\hat{O}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{1}_{\text{s}}
for both even and odd
\hat{O}_{\text{e}}
, while
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}}=\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
if
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
is odd, and
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}}=\hat{1}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
if
\hat{O}_{\text{s}}
is even.
From this, the fermionic extension in the main text
Eq.

3.39
naturally follows.

Appendix B
Proof of Eq.
2.52
in the fermionic case

In this appendix, we prove
Eq.

2.52
in the fermionic case, which we restate here for convenience:

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{E}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{0}t}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\int_{0}^{t}\cdots\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}s_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}s_{m}\bm{\mathcal{T}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha_{i}}\left(t_{i}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{L}_{\mathrm{SE}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}(0)\right)

(B.1)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} =(-1)^{\sigma_{t}}&\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{s}}t}}{m!}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s_{m}\\
&\qquad\qquad\sum_{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{m}=1}^{N}C^{(m)}_{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{m}}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{m})\mathbf{T}^{\pm}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}{\bm{S}}_{\alpha_{i}}(t_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{S}_{\beta_{j}}(s_{j})\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{S}}(0),\end{aligned}

where
\sigma_{t}
is the permutation of
1,\cdots,2n
such that

t_{\sigma_{t}(1)}\geq t_{\sigma_{t}(2)}\leq\cdots\geq t_{\sigma_{t}(2n)}
. In other words, we need to introduce a definition of
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha}
in the fermionic case, such that
\|\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha}\|_{\text{tr}}=1
and
Eq.

B.1
holds.
To achieve this, let us first introduce the environment parity superoperator
\bm{P}_{\text{e}}
, defined as follows:

\bm{P}_{\text{e}}:\hat{\rho}\rightarrow\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}.

(B.2)

where
\hat{P}_{\text{e}}
is the environment parity operators as defined in
Eq.

3.30
.
Therefore, we have for any
\hat{\rho}\in B_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}})
,

\bm{c}_{k}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}=\bm{c}_{k}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}\hat{P}_{\text{e}})=\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}=-\hat{c}_{k}\hat{\rho}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}=-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}\hat{\rho})\hat{P}_{\text{e}}=-\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\bm{c}_{k}\hat{\rho},

\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}=\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}\hat{P}_{\text{e}})=\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}=-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}=-\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}\hat{\rho},

where
\bm{c}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}
are defined in
Eq.

3.31
.
In other words,

\{\bm{c}_{k},\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\}=0
and
\{\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k},\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\}=0
.
Similarly,

\{\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger},\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\}=0
and
\{\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger},\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\}=0
.
Since
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
is quadratic in
\bm{c}_{k},\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}
, we have
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}=\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
.
Then, let us define the superoperators
\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha}
as follows:

\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha}=\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\bm{S}_{\alpha}.

(B.3)

Since
\|\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\|_{\text{tr}}=1
, we have
\|\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha}\|_{\text{tr}}=1
.
Without loss of generality, let us assume that
t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq\cdots\geq t_{2n}
.
For
s_{1},\cdots,s_{m}\in[0,T]
, let
\sigma_{s}

be the permutation of
1,\cdots,m
such that
s_{\sigma_{s}(1)}\geq s_{\sigma_{s}(2)}\geq\cdots\geq s_{\sigma_{s}(m)}
.
For
t_{1},\cdots,t_{2n}\in[0,t]

and
s_{1},\cdots,s_{m}\in[0,t]
, let
w_{i}=t_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,2n
) and
w_{2n+i}=s_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,m
). Let
us define permutation
\widetilde{\sigma}\in S_{2n+m}
such that
w_{\widetilde{\sigma}(1)}\geq w_{\widetilde{\sigma}(2)}\geq\cdots\geq w_{\widetilde{\sigma}(2n+m)}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{0}t}\bm{\mathcal{T}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha_{i}}\left(t_{i}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{L}_{\text{SE}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}(0)

(B.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{0}t}\sum_{\beta_{1},\cdots\beta_{m}=1}^{N}(-1)^{\widetilde{\sigma}}\bm{\mathcal{T}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}(t_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\otimes(-1)^{\widetilde{\sigma}}\bm{\mathcal{T}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha_{i}}\left(t_{i}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{S}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{0}t}\sum_{\beta_{1},\cdots\beta_{m}=1}^{N}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}(t_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\otimes\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha_{i}}\left(t_{i}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{S}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta_{1},\cdots\beta_{m}=1}^{N}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}(t_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{e}}^{(0)}\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{s}}t}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\widetilde{\bm{S}}_{\alpha_{i}}\left(t_{i}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{S}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{s}}^{(0)}.

Here
\bm{P}_{\text{e}}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}=\bm{P}_{\text{e}}
since
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
and
\bm{P}_{\text{e}}
commute, and
t_{i}
serves as a time argument for performing the time-ordering operation.
Let us define
\sigma_{s}\in S_{m}
such that
s_{\sigma_{s}(1)}\geq s_{\sigma_{s}(2)}\geq\cdots\geq s_{\sigma_{s}(m)}
.
Furthermore, let us define
\sigma_{st}
as the permutation of
1,\cdots,2n+m
such that if letting
w_{1}=t_{1},\cdots,w_{2n}=t_{2n},w_{2n+1}=s_{1},\cdots,w_{2n+m}=s_{m}
,
then
w_{\sigma_{st}(1)}\geq w_{\sigma_{st}(2)}\geq\cdots\geq w_{\sigma_{st}(2n+m)}
.
Since
t_{1}\geq\cdots t_{2n}
, we have

(-1)^{\widetilde{\sigma}}=(-1)^{\sigma_{s}}(-1)^{\sigma_{st}}
.
Let
n_{0}
be the number of
i
such that
t_{i}\in[s_{\sigma_{s}(1)},t]
,

n_{m}
be the number of
i
such that
t_{i}\in[0,s_{\sigma_{s}(m)})
, and
n_{j}
be the number of
i
such that
t_{i}\in[s_{\sigma_{s}(j)},s_{\sigma_{s}(j+1)})
for
j=1,\cdots,m-1
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\bm{P}_{\text{e}}(t_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{e}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}(-1)^{\widetilde{\sigma}}(\bm{P}_{\text{e}})^{n_{0}}\bm{E}_{\beta_{\sigma_{s}(1)}}\left(s_{\sigma_{s}(1)}\right)(\bm{P}_{\text{e}})^{n_{1}}\cdots\bm{E}_{\beta_{\sigma_{s}(m)}}\left(s_{\sigma_{s}(m)}\right)(\bm{P}_{\text{e}})^{n_{m}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{e}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}(-1)^{\sigma_{s}}(\bm{P}_{\text{e}})^{2n}\bm{E}_{\beta_{\sigma_{s}(1)}}\left(s_{\sigma_{s}(1)}\right)\cdots\bm{E}_{\beta_{\sigma_{s}(m)}}\left(s_{\sigma_{s}(m)}\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{e}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}(-1)^{\sigma_{s}}\bm{E}_{\beta_{\sigma_{s}(1)}}\left(s_{\sigma_{s}(1)}\right)\cdots\bm{E}_{\beta_{\sigma_{s}(m)}}\left(s_{\sigma_{s}(m)}\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{e}}^{(0)}=\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}_{\text{e}}^{(0)}.

Here we have used the anti-commutation relation
\{\bm{P}_{\text{e}},\bm{E}_{\beta}\}=0
for all
\beta=1,\cdots,N
and
(\bm{P}_{\text{e}})^{2}=\bm{1}_{\text{e}}
.
Then
we have

\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}t}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2n}\bm{P}_{\mathrm{e}}\left(t_{i}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}_{\mathrm{e}}^{(0)}\right)=\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}t}\mathbf{T}^{-}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bm{E}_{\beta_{j}}\left(s_{j}\right)\right)\hat{\rho}_{\mathrm{e}}^{(0)}\right)=C^{(m)}_{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{m}}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{m}).

Therefore,
Eq.

B.1
holds.

Appendix C
Liouvillians and BCFs

The main goal of this section is to give a proof for
Lemma

3.11
and
Lemma

3.16
, from which the reduced system density dynamics (see
Corollary

3.10
,
Corollary

3.15
) and the error bound (see
Corollary

3.12
,
Eq.

3.53
) follow naturally.
We first introduce several useful lemmas. Then, in
Section

C.1
, we prove
Lemma

3.11
for unitary, Lindblad, and quasi-Lindblad cases. In
Section

C.2
, we first give the explicit expressions for
\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{D}\mp}
in
Eq.

4.4
, then we prove
Lemma

3.16
also for each cases.

Some useful lemmas

Let us introduce the shorthand notation
\langle\bm{O}\rangle_{\text{e}}:=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{O}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))
for
\bm{O}
acting on
B_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\text{e}})
, and
\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{\text{e}}:=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{O}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))
for
\hat{O}
acting on
\mathcal{H}_{\text{e}}
.
For any two environment superoperators
\bm{O}_{1},\bm{O}_{2}
, and
t>t_{1}>t_{2}>0
, consider the following two-point correlation functions:

\operatorname{tr}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}(t-t_{1})}\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}(t_{1}-t_{2})}\bm{O}_{2}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t_{2}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)).

(C.1)

Since
\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)
is the stationary state of
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
and
\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}
is a trace-preserving map, then the above correlation function is equal to

\langle\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}(t_{1}-t_{2})}\bm{O}_{2}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}(t_{1}-t_{2})}\bm{O}_{2}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)),

which only relies on
(t_{1}-t_{2})
.
Thus let us take
(t_{1},t_{2})=(t,0)
. Then taking derivative with respect to
t
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\bm{O}_{2}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\bm{L}_{\text{e}}\bm{O}_{2}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}[\bm{L}_{\text{e}},\bm{O}_{2}]\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))+\operatorname{tr}(\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\bm{O}_{2}\bm{L}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{O}_{1}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}[\bm{L}_{\text{e}},\bm{O}_{2}]\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)),

where we have used again that
\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)
is the stationary state of
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
.

In the unitary case, the following results are well-known and useful (for example, see
[
NO98
]
):

Lemma C.1
.

In the bosonic case, let
\hat{H}_{\text{e}}=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}H_{kk^{\prime}}\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{k^{\prime}}
. With environment density operator being
Eq.

3.4
,
we have

\langle\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{k}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\left((\mathrm{e}^{\beta H}-I)^{-1}\right)_{kl},\quad\langle\hat{b}_{k}\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\rangle_{\text{e}}=((I-\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H})^{-1})_{kl},

Similarly, in the fermionic cases, by replacing
\hat{b}_{k},\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}
with fermionic operators
\hat{c}_{k},\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}
, we have

\langle\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{c}_{k}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\left((\mathrm{e}^{\beta H}+I)^{-1}\right)_{kl},\quad\langle\hat{c}_{k}\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\rangle_{\text{e}}=((I+\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H})^{-1})_{kl}.

For both bosonic and fermionic
\hat{H}_{\text{e}}
, we
will also use the following commutator relations:

Lemma C.2
.

In the unitary case, with
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}^{\text{U}}
in
Eq.

3.3
and
Eq.

3.33
, we have:

[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}^{\text{U}}]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\mathrm{i}H_{kl}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k},\quad[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}^{\text{U}}]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}.

(C.2)

where
\mathbf{b}_{k},\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}
are understood to be
\bm{b}_{k},\bm{b}_{k}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}
in the bosonic case, and

\bm{c}_{k},\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}
in the fermionic case.
We also have:

[\mathbf{b}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0,\quad[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}]=0,\quad[\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}]=-\delta_{k^{\prime}l}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k},\quad[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}]=\mp\delta_{kl}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}.

[\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]=0,\quad[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]=0,\quad[\mathbf{b}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]=\pm\delta_{kl}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger},\quad[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]=\delta_{k^{\prime}l}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}.

Proof.

Let us introduce the notation
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mp}
, where
[\cdot,\cdot]_{-}=[\cdot,\cdot]
and
[\cdot,\cdot]_{+}=\{\cdot,\cdot\}
.
Using the CCR relation
Eq.

3.2
and the CAR relation
Eq.

3.32
, we have
[{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
,
[{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
,
[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
,
[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
,
[{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
,
[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
,
[{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]=0
,
[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]=0
,
and

\displaystyle[{\mathbf{b}}_{l},{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=[{\mathbf{b}}_{l},{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}\pm{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}[{\mathbf{b}}_{l},{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]_{\mp}=\delta_{lk}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}},\quad[\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}\bm{{\mathbf{b}}}_{k^{\prime}}]=[\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}\pm\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}[\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]_{\mp}=-\delta_{lk^{\prime}}\bm{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}.

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}\pm\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=\delta_{lk}\widetilde{{\mathbf{b}}}_{k^{\prime}},\quad[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}\pm\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}]=-\delta_{lk^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}.

With
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}^{\text{U}}=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}-\mathrm{i}H_{kk^{\prime}}{\mathbf{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}+\mathrm{i}H_{kk^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}
,
Eq.

C.2
is proved.
Furthermore, we have

[\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}]=\pm\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\dagger}=-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}[\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}},\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}=-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\delta_{k^{\prime}l},

[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}]=\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}\mp\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}=\mp[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}\mathbf{b}_{k^{\prime}}=\mp\delta_{kl}{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}},

[\mathbf{b}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}]=\pm\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{l}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{l}=\pm\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}[\mathbf{b}_{l},\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}=\pm\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\delta_{lk},

[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}]=\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\mp\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}=[\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{l},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}]_{\mp}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}=\delta_{k^{\prime}l}\mathbf{b}_{k}^{\dagger}.

∎

C.1
Spin-boson model

We have established the commutator relations in the unitary case in Lemma
C.2
. Let us also state the commutator result for the Lindbladian case.

Lemma C.3
.

With
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}=\bm{L}_{\text{e}}^{\text{U}}+\bm{D}_{\text{e}}
defined in
Eq.

3.3
, we have:

\displaystyle[\bm{b}_{l},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}-\Gamma_{lk})\bm{b}_{k},\quad[\bm{b}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(\mathrm{i}H_{kl}+\Gamma_{kl})\bm{b}_{k}^{\dagger}-2\Gamma_{kl}\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k},

(C.3)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{l},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(\mathrm{i}H_{kl}-\Gamma_{kl})\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k},\quad[\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}+\Gamma_{lk})\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}-2\Gamma_{lk}{\bm{b}}_{k}.

The proof of this lemma follows naturally from Lemma
C.2
.

Now let us prove
Lemma

3.11
. Let us first consider the unitary case. We have the following lemma:

Lemma C.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)
be a matrix-valued function, with its
(k,l)
-th element being
\langle{\bm{b}_{k}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\bm{b}_{l}^{\dagger}}\rangle_{\text{e}}
for
t\geq 0
. Similarly, let us define
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
where
\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}\in\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}^{\dagger}}
. Then, in the unitary case (see
Eqs.

3.3
and
3.33
), the nonzero components of
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
(
t\geq 0
) are:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)=G^{>}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}(t)=C_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}}(t)=(G^{<}(t))^{*},

(C.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)=G^{<}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=(G^{>}(t))^{*},

where we have defined in
Eq.

3.22
that,

G^{<}(t)=\left(\mathrm{e}^{\beta H}-I\right)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}Ht},\quad G^{>}(t)=\left(I-\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H}\right)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}Ht}.

(C.5)

Proof.

Since
(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}}(0))_{kl}=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{b}_{k}\bm{b}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{k}\hat{b}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=0
, and using
Lemma

C.2
, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}})_{kl}=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\mathrm{i}H_{lk^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}})_{kk^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
C_{\bm{b},\bm{b}}(t)=0
for all
t>0
.
Similarly, we have
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)=0
,
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)=0
,
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=0
,
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=0
,
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)=0
,

\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)=0
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}(t)=0
. We also have:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{k}\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=\langle\hat{b}_{k}\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{b}_{k})=(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl},

(C.6)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=\langle\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{l}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})=(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}}(0))_{kl},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{b}_{l}\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})=\langle\hat{b}_{l}\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{b}_{l})=(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{b}_{k}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger})=\langle\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{k}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{k})=(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(0))_{kl}.

Or in matrix form,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(0)=(I-\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H})^{-1},\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}}(0)=\left(\mathrm{e}^{\beta H^{\mathrm{T}}}-I\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(0)=\left(\mathrm{e}^{\beta H}-I\right)^{-1},\quad\mathcal{C}_{{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(0)=\left(I-\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H^{\mathrm{T}}}\right)^{-1},

and

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}})_{kl}=\operatorname{tr}\left(\bm{b}_{k}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}t}\left[\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}},\bm{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\right]\hat{\rho}_{\mathrm{E}}(0)\right)=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}-\mathrm{i}H_{k^{\prime}l}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}})_{kk^{\prime}},

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}})_{kl}=\operatorname{tr}\left(\bm{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\left[\bm{L}_{\text{e}},\bm{b}_{l}\right]\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\right)=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\mathrm{i}H_{lk^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}})_{kk^{\prime}},

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}})_{kl}=\operatorname{tr}\left(\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k}^{\dagger}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\left[\bm{L}_{\text{e}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{l}\right]\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\right)=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}-\mathrm{i}H_{k^{\prime}l}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}})_{kk^{\prime}},

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}})_{kl}=\operatorname{tr}\left(\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{k}\mathrm{e}^{\bm{L}_{\text{e}}t}\left[\bm{L}_{\text{e}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{l}^{\dagger}\right]\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\right)=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\mathrm{i}H_{lk^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}})_{kk^{\prime}}.

Or in matrix form

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}=-\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}H,\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}=\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}H^{\mathrm{T}},\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}=-\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}H,\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}=\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}H^{\mathrm{T}}.

And similarly we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}=-\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}H,\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}}=\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}}H^{\mathrm{T}},\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}=-\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}H,\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{{\bm{b}^{\dagger}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}=\mathrm{i}\mathcal{C}_{{\bm{b}^{\dagger}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}H^{\mathrm{T}}.

And thus, the lemma is proved.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

3.11
, in the unitary case.

Using
Eq.

3.7
,
we have

C_{2i-1,2j-1}(t)=-\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}g_{ik}g_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}+g_{ik}^{*}g_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}}(t))_{kl}=-(gG^{>}(t)g^{\dagger}+(gG^{<}(t)g^{\dagger})^{*})_{ij},

C_{2i-1,2j}(t)=\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}g_{ik}g_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t))_{kl}+g_{ik}^{*}g_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}=(gG^{<}(t)g^{\dagger}+(gG^{>}(t)g^{\dagger})^{*})_{ij},

C_{2i,2j-1}(t)=\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}g_{ik}g_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}+g_{ik}^{*}g_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\bm{b}}(t))_{kl}=(gG^{>}(t)g^{\dagger}+(gG^{<}(t)g^{\dagger})^{*})_{ij},

C_{2i,2j}(t)=-\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}g_{ik}g_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t))_{kl}+g_{ik}^{*}g_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}=-(gG^{<}(t)g^{\dagger}+(gG^{>}(t)g^{\dagger})^{*})_{ij}.

Let
c_{ij}(t)=-C_{2i-1,2j-1}(t)
, then
C_{2i,2j-1}(t)=c_{ij}(t)
,
C_{2i,2j}(t)=-(c_{ij}(t))^{*}
, and
C_{2i-1,2j}(t)=(c_{ij}(t))^{*}
.
∎

Next, we turn to the Lindblad case:

Proof of
Lemma

3.11
, in the Lindblad case.

Let us define
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}
the same as in
Eq.

C.5
for
\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}=\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}
. Since
\hat{\rho}_{\mathrm{E}}^{(0)}=|0\rangle_{\mathrm{E}}\left\langle\left.0\right|_{\mathrm{E}}\right.
, then

\langle b_{k}\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\delta_{kl},\quad\langle\hat{b}_{l}^{\dagger}b_{k}\rangle_{\text{e}}=0.

Thus using
Eq.

C.6
, we know that the only nonzero ones among all
C_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(0)
, (
\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}=\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}
) are:

{(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(0))}_{kl}=(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}=(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}=(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}=\delta_{kl}.

Using the result for
[\bm{b}_{l},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}]
and
[\widetilde{\bm{b}}_{l},\bm{L}_{\text{e}}]
in the above lemma, similar to
Eq.

C.5
, we have:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}}=C_{\bm{O},\bm{b}}(\mathrm{i}H^{\text{T}}+\Gamma^{\text{T}}),\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}=C_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(-\mathrm{i}H+\Gamma),\quad\text{for }\bm{O}=\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}.

Note that
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(0)=0
, thus
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)=0
for all
t
and
\bm{O}=\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}
.
For the rest, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)+2\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}\Gamma,\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(\mathrm{i}H^{\text{T}}-\Gamma^{\text{T}})+2\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}}\Gamma^{\text{T}}.

Using
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{b}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)=0
and the initial conditions, we know that
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=0
for all
t
, and

\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t},\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(\mathrm{i}H^{\text{T}}-\Gamma^{\text{T}})t}=(\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t})^{*}.

(C.7)

Thus

\displaystyle C_{2i-1,2j-1}(t)=-g\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)g^{\dagger}=-g\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}g^{\dagger},\quad C_{2i-1,2j}(t)=g^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)g^{\text{T}}=(g\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}g^{\dagger})^{*},

\displaystyle C_{2i,2j-1}(t)=g\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)g^{\dagger}=g\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}g^{\dagger},\quad C_{2i,2j}(t)=-g^{*}\mathrm{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)g^{\text{T}}=-(g\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}g^{\dagger})^{*}.

Let
c(t)=g\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}g^{\dagger}
, then
C_{2i-1,2j-1}(t)=-c_{ij}(t)
,

C_{2i,2j-1}(t)=c_{ij}(t)
,

C_{2i-1,2j}(t)=(c_{ij}(t))^{*}
,

C_{2i,2j}(t)=-(c_{ij}(t))^{*}
.
∎

Finally, let us prove this result for quasi-Lindblad dynamics:

Proof of
Lemma

4.2
, in the bosonic case.

Recall that in the quasi-Lindblad case,
\bm{E}_{\alpha}=\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{U}}+\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{D}}
, where
\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{U}}
and
\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{D}}
are defined in
Eq.

3.7

and
Eq.

4.2
respectively.
Since
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
is the same in the Lindblad and quasi-Lindblad case,
the result of
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
(see
Eq.

C.7
) still holds, and we have

C_{2i-1,2j-1}(t)=((-\mathrm{i}g-M)\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)(-\mathrm{i}g^{\dagger}-M^{\dagger}))_{ij}=-((g-\mathrm{i}M)\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}(g^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}M^{\dagger}))_{ij}

\displaystyle C_{2i-1,2j}(t)

\displaystyle=((-\mathrm{i}g^{*}-M^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)(\mathrm{i}g^{\text{T}}-M^{\text{T}})+2M^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)(\mathrm{i}g^{\text{T}}-M^{\text{T}}))_{ij}

\displaystyle=((-\mathrm{i}g^{*}+M^{*})\mathrm{e}^{\left(\mathrm{i}H^{\mathrm{T}}-\Gamma^{\mathrm{T}}\right)t}(\mathrm{i}g^{\text{T}}-M^{\text{T}}))_{ij}=(((g-\mathrm{i}M)\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}(g^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}M^{\dagger}))_{ij})^{*},

\displaystyle C_{2i,2j-1}(t)

\displaystyle=((\mathrm{i}g-M)\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger},\bm{b}^{\dagger}}(t)(-\mathrm{i}g^{\dagger}-M^{\dagger})+2M\mathcal{C}_{\bm{b},\widetilde{\bm{b}}}(t)(-\mathrm{i}g^{\dagger}-M^{\dagger}))_{ij}

\displaystyle=\left((g-\mathrm{i}M)\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}\left(g^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}M^{\dagger}\right)\right)_{ij}

C_{2i,2j}(t)=((\mathrm{i}g^{*}-M^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}^{\dagger}}(t)(\mathrm{i}g^{\text{T}}-M^{\text{T}}))_{ij}=-(((g-\mathrm{i}M)\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}(g^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}M^{\dagger}))_{ij})^{*}.

Let
c(t)=(g-\mathrm{i}M)\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H-\Gamma)t}\left(g^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}M^{\dagger}\right)
, then
C_{2i-1,2j-1}(t)=-c_{ij}(t)
,

C_{2i,2j-1}(t)=c_{ij}(t)
,

C_{2i-1,2j}(t)=(c_{ij}(t))^{*}
,

C_{2i,2j}(t)=-(c_{ij}(t))^{*}
.
∎

C.2
Fermionic impurity model

Let us first give the explicit expressions for
\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{D}\mp}
in
Eq.

4.4
:

\displaystyle\bm{E}_{4j-3}^{\text{D}-}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}-M_{jk}\bm{c}_{k},\quad\bm{E}_{4j-2}^{\text{D}-}=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}-M_{jk}^{*}\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger}+2M_{jk}^{*}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k},\quad\bm{E}_{4j-1}^{\text{D}-}=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}-M_{jk}^{*}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k},

(C.8)

\displaystyle\bm{E}_{4j}^{\text{D}-}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}-M_{jk}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}-2M_{jk}\bm{c}_{k},\quad\bm{E}_{4j-3}^{\text{D}+}=\sum_{l=N_{1}+1}^{N}M_{jl}\bm{c}_{l}+2M_{jl}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l}^{\dagger},\quad\bm{E}_{4j-2}^{\text{D}+}=\sum_{l=N_{1}+1}^{N_{\text{e}}}M_{jl}^{*}\bm{c}_{l}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\bm{E}_{4j-1}^{\text{D}+}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=N_{1}+1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(M_{jl})^{*}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l}-2(M_{jl})^{*}\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger},\quad\bm{E}_{4j}^{\text{D}+}=\sum_{l=N_{1}+1}^{N_{\text{e}}}M_{jl}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l}^{\dagger}.

Now let us prove that with
\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{U}}
in

Eq.

3.45
and
\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{D}\mp}
in
Eq.

C.8
, the operator form and the superoperator form of
\bm{L}_{\text{SE}}^{\text{U}}
and
\bm{D}_{\text{SE}}^{\mp}
are equivalent. The key is to take advantage of the fermionic superselection rule to simplify the formulation of
\bm{L}_{\text{SE}}
in the superoperator formalism.

Lemma C.5
.

Due to the fermionic superselection rule (see
Eq.

3.42
),
\bm{L}_{\text{SE}}^{\text{U}}
in
Eq.

3.43
and
Eq.

3.41
are equivalent, and
\bm{D}_{\text{SE}}^{\mp}
in
Eq.

4.4
and
Eq.

4.3
are equivalent.

Proof.

Using
Eq.

3.39
, we have

\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}\left(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}\right)\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger}+\nu_{ik}^{*}\left(\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\right)\otimes\hat{a}_{i}.

Then

\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}\hat{\rho}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(\nu_{ik}((\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k})\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger})\hat{\rho}-\nu_{ik}^{*}((\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger})\otimes\hat{a}_{i})\hat{\rho})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(\nu_{ik}\bm{c}_{k}\otimes\bm{a}_{i}^{\dagger}+\nu_{ik}^{*}\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\bm{a}_{i})\hat{\rho}.

From this, one can find the values of
\bm{E}^{U}_{4i-3}
,
\bm{E}^{U}_{4i-2}
,
\bm{S}_{4i-3}
,
\bm{S}_{4i-2}
.
Multiplying
\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}
from the right, we have

\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}\hat{\rho}((-\hat{c}_{k}\hat{P}_{\text{e}})\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger})+\nu_{ik}^{*}\hat{\rho}((\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}})\otimes\hat{a}_{i}).

As a result of the fermionic superselection rule
Eq.

3.42
, we only need to consider
\hat{\rho}
in the physical space
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}}))
, namely,
[\hat{P},\hat{\rho}]=0
. Since
[\hat{P},\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}]=0
, we have
[\hat{P},\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}]=[\hat{P},\hat{\rho}]\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}+\hat{\rho}[\hat{P},\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}]=0
. In other words,

\hat{P}\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}=\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}\hat{P}
.
Multiplying
\hat{P}
from the right, we have
\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}=\hat{P}\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}\hat{P}
, and therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}\hat{H}_{\text{SE}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}((-\hat{c}_{k}\hat{P}_{\text{e}})\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger})(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})+\nu_{ik}^{*}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}((\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}})\otimes\hat{a}_{i})(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}(\hat{c}_{k}\otimes(-\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{s}}))+\nu_{ik}^{*}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}(\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes(\hat{a}_{i}\hat{P}_{\text{s}}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}(\nu_{ik}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{i}+\nu_{ik}^{*}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}\otimes\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{i}^{\dagger})\hat{\rho}.

Combining the above equalities one can see
\bm{L}_{\text{SE}}^{\text{U}}
in
Eq.

3.43
and
Eq.

3.41
are equivalent.
Furthermore,
with
Eq.

3.31
, we have

\hat{a}_{i}|_{\mathcal{H}}\hat{\rho}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}|_{\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{a}_{i})\hat{\rho}(\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\hat{I}_{\text{s}})=(\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}\otimes\bm{a}_{i})\hat{\rho},\quad\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger}|_{\mathcal{H}}\hat{\rho}\hat{c}_{k}|_{\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger})\hat{\rho}(\hat{c}_{k}\otimes\hat{I}_{\text{s}})=(\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\bm{a}_{i}^{\dagger})\hat{\rho}.

And since
\hat{\rho}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}_{\text{E}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\text{S}})
, we have

\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}|_{\mathcal{H}}\hat{\rho}\hat{a}_{i}|_{\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\hat{I}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{a}_{i})=(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})(\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\hat{I}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{a}_{i})(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})=-(\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\otimes\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{i}^{\dagger})\hat{\rho},

\hat{c}_{k}|_{\mathcal{H}}\hat{\rho}\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger}|_{\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{c}_{k}\otimes\hat{I}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger})=(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})(\hat{c}_{k}\otimes\hat{I}_{\text{s}})\hat{\rho}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{a}_{i}^{\dagger})(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\otimes\hat{P}_{\text{s}})=-(\bm{c}_{k}\otimes\widetilde{\bm{a}}_{i})\hat{\rho}.

Thus,
\bm{D}_{\text{SE}}^{\mp}
in
Eq.

4.4
and
Eq.

4.3
are equivalent.
∎

Then
similar to
Eq.

C.5
, in the unitary fermionic impurity model, we have:

Lemma C.6
.

Let us define
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
the same as in
Eq.

C.5
, where
\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}=\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}
. Then, in the unitary case, the nonzero components of
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
(for
t\geq 0
) are:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t)=G^{>}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}(t)=C_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}(t)=(G^{<}(t))^{*},

(C.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)=G^{<}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)=(G^{>}(t))^{*}.

where we have defind in
Eq.

3.49
that,

G^{<}(t)=\left(\mathrm{e}^{\beta H}+I\right)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}Ht},\quad G^{>}(t)=\left(I+\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H}\right)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}Ht}.

The proof of
Lemma

C.6
is similar to that of
Eq.

C.5
, and we only emphasize the key difference:

Proof.

Using
Lemma

C.2
, similar to
Eq.

C.5
, the only nonzero components would be
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}(t)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)
,
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t)
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}(t)
. Their initial values, however, are different from those in
Eq.

C.5
because of the parity operator in the definition of the fermionic superoperators
Eq.

3.31
. We have:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{c}_{k}\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=-\operatorname{tr}(-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{k})=-(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{c}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0))=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{l}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger})=(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}(0))_{kl},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{l}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger})=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{l}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger})=\operatorname{tr}(-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{l})=(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(0))_{kl},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(0))_{kl}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{c}_{k})=\operatorname{tr}(\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\hat{c}_{k})=\operatorname{tr}(-\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}(0)\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger})=-(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(0))_{kl}.

Here, we have repeatedly used that
\hat{P}_{\text{e}}\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}=-\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{P}_{\text{e}}
, and
\hat{P}_{\text{e}}^{2}=\hat{I}_{\text{e}}
. In matrix form, the above becomes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(0)=-\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(0)=(I+\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H})^{-1},\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}(0)=\left(\mathrm{e}^{\beta H^{\mathrm{T}}}+I\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(0)=-\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(0)=\left(\mathrm{e}^{\beta H}+I\right)^{-1},\quad\mathcal{C}_{{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(0)=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(0)=\left(I+\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H^{\mathrm{T}}}\right)^{-1}.

The equation of motion for these correlation functions is the same as in
Eq.

C.5
, and thus the lemma is proved.
∎

Now let us prove
Lemma

3.16
in the unitary case.

Proof of
Lemma

3.16
, in the unitary case.

Recall that
\bm{E}_{\alpha}=\bm{E}_{\alpha}^{\text{U}}
is defined in
Eq.

3.45
. Then
we have the following nonzero components of the correlation functions (for
t\geq 0
):

C_{4i-3,4j-2}(t)=-\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}\nu_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}=-(\nu G^{>}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij},

C_{4i-3,4j-1}(t)=\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}\nu_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t))_{kl}=-(\nu G^{<}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij},

C_{4i-2,4j-3}(t)=-\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}^{*}\nu_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}(t))_{kl}=-((\nu G^{<}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij})^{*},

C_{4i-2,4j}(t)=\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}^{*}\nu_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}=((\nu G^{>}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij})^{*},

C_{4i-1,4j-3}(t)=\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}^{*}\nu_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}(t))_{kl}=((\nu G^{<}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij})^{*},

C_{4i-1,4j}(t)=-\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}^{*}\nu_{jl}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}=-((\nu G^{>}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij})^{*},

C_{4i,4j-2}(t)=\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}\nu_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t))_{kl}=-(\nu G^{>}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij},

C_{4i,4j-1}(t)=-\sum_{k,l=1}^{N_{\text{e}}}\nu_{ik}\nu_{jl}^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t))_{kl}=-(\nu G^{<}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij}.

Let us define

\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t)=(\nu G^{>}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij},\quad\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t)=(\nu G^{<}(t)\nu^{\dagger})_{ij}.

Thus,

\displaystyle C_{4i-3,4j-2}(t)=C_{4i,4j-2}(t)=-\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t),\quad

\displaystyle C_{4i-3,4j-1}(t)=C_{4i,4j-1}(t)=-\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t),

\displaystyle C_{4i-2,4j}(t)=-C_{4i-1,4j}(t)=(\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t))^{*},\quad

\displaystyle C_{4i-1,4j-3}(t)=-C_{4i-2,4j-3}(t)=(\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t))^{*}.

∎

For the Lindblad and quasi-Lindblad fermionic impurity model, as a result of
Lemma

C.2
, with
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}=\bm{L}_{\text{e}}^{\text{U}}+\bm{D}_{\text{e}}
(see
Eq.

3.43
,
Eq.

4.4
), we have the following commutator result.
For
l=1,\ldots,N_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left[\bm{c}_{l},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}\left(-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}^{-}-\Gamma_{lk}^{-}\right)\bm{c}_{k},\quad\left[\bm{c}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}\left(\mathrm{i}H_{kl}^{-}+\Gamma_{kl}^{-}\right)\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger}-2\Gamma_{kl}^{-}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left[\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{l=1}^{N_{1}}\left(\mathrm{i}H_{kl}^{-}-\Gamma_{kl}^{-}\right)\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k},\quad\left[\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{1}}\left(-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}^{-}+\Gamma_{lk}^{-}\right)\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}+2\Gamma_{lk}^{-}\bm{c}_{k}},

and for
l=N_{1}+1,\ldots,N_{\text{e}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\bm{c}_{l},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=N_{1}+1}^{N}\left(-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}^{+}+\Gamma_{lk}^{+}\right)\bm{c}_{k}+2\Gamma_{lk}^{+}\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger},\quad\left[\bm{c}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=N_{1}+1}^{N}\left(\mathrm{i}H_{kl}^{+}-\Gamma_{kl}^{-}\right)\bm{c}_{k}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\left[\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=N_{1}+1}^{N}\left(\mathrm{i}H_{kl}^{+}+\Gamma_{kl}^{+}\right)\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}-2\Gamma_{kl}^{+}{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger},\quad\left[\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{l}^{\dagger},\bm{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\right]=\sum_{k=N_{1}+1}^{N}\left(-\mathrm{i}H_{lk}^{+}-\Gamma_{lk}^{+}\right)\widetilde{\bm{c}}_{k}^{\dagger}.

Now we are ready to calculate the BCFs for Lindblad and quasi-Lindblad dynamics.

Proof of
Lemma

3.16
, in the Lindblad case.

Let us define
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
the same as in
Lemma

C.6
for
\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}=\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}
.
Let us define
\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
,

\mathcal{C}^{-+}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
,

\mathcal{C}^{+-}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
, and
\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
as the subblocks of
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
:

\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)&\mathcal{C}^{-+}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)\\
\mathcal{C}^{+-}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)&\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)\end{pmatrix}.

\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{C}^{-+}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{C}^{+-}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}
are of size
N_{1}\times N_{1}
,
N_{1}\times(N_{\text{e}}-N_{1})
,
(N_{\text{e}}-N_{1})\times N_{1}
, and
(N_{\text{e}}-N_{1})\times(N_{\text{e}}-N_{1})
, respectively.
Since initial density operator is
\hat{\rho}_{\text{E}}^{(0)}=(\otimes_{k=1}^{N_{1}}|0\rangle_{k}\langle 0|)\otimes(\otimes_{k=N_{1}+1}^{N_{\text{e}}}|1\rangle_{k}\langle 1|)
, thus

\langle\hat{c}_{k}\hat{c}_{l}^{\dagger}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\delta_{kl},&1\leq k,l\leq N_{1},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.,\quad\langle\hat{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{c}_{l}\rangle_{\text{e}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\delta_{kl},&N_{1}+1\leq k,l\leq N_{\text{e}},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right..

Thus

\mathcal{C}^{-+}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(0)=\mathcal{C}^{+-}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(0)=0
for all
\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}=\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}
. As a result,
\mathcal{C}^{-+}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)=\mathcal{C}^{+-}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)=0
for all
t\geq 0
.
Since
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}^{--}(t)
has the same initial value as
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
in the proof of
Eq.

3.7
, then using the same argument, the only nonzero components are

\mathrm{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)=-\mathrm{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H^{-}-\Gamma^{-})t},\quad\mathrm{C}^{--}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathrm{C}^{--}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{\left(\mathrm{i}(H^{-})^{\mathrm{T}}-(\Gamma^{-})^{\mathrm{T}}\right)t}.

(C.10)

With a similar argument,for
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}^{++}
, the only nonzero components are

-\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}^{++}(t)=\mathrm{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}^{++}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H^{+}-\Gamma^{+})t},\quad\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}^{++}(t)=\mathrm{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}^{++}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(\mathrm{i}(H^{+})^{\text{T}}-(\Gamma^{+})^{\text{T}})t},

(C.11)

Then let us define

\mathcal{G}^{>}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H^{-}-\Gamma^{-})t}
,
\mathcal{G}^{<}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{(-\mathrm{i}H^{+}-\Gamma^{+})t}
, and
\Delta^{>}(t)=\nu^{-}\mathcal{G}^{>}(t)(\nu^{-})^{\dagger}
,
\Delta^{<}(t)=\nu^{+}\mathcal{G}^{<}(t)(\nu^{+})^{\dagger}
, where
\nu^{-},\nu^{+}
are
subblocks of matrix
\nu
of size
n\times N_{1}
and
n\times(N_{\text{e}}-N_{1})
, respectively, i.e.
\nu=\begin{pmatrix}\nu^{-},\nu^{+}\end{pmatrix}
. Then we have:

\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{4i-3,4j-2}(t)=C_{4i,4j-2}(t)=-\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t),&C_{4i-3,4j-1}(t)=C_{4i,4j-1}(t)=-\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t)\\
C_{4i-2,4j}(t)=-C_{4i-1,4j}(t)=\left(\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t)\right)^{*},&C_{4i-1,4j-3}(t)=-C_{4i-2,4j-3}(t)=\left(\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t)\right)^{*}.\end{array}

∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.2
, in the fermionic case.

Let us define
\mathcal{G}^{>}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{\left(-\mathrm{i}H^{-}-\Gamma^{-}\right)t},\mathcal{G}^{<}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{\left(-\mathrm{i}H^{+}-\Gamma^{+}\right)t}
, and

\Delta^{>}(t)=\left(\nu^{-}-\mathrm{i}M^{-}\right)\mathcal{G}^{>}(t)\left(\left(\nu^{-}\right)^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}\left(M^{-}\right)^{\dagger}\right),\quad\Delta^{<}(t)=\left(\nu^{+}-\mathrm{i}M^{+}\right)\mathcal{G}^{<}(t)\left(\nu^{+}-\mathrm{i}M^{+}\right).

Since
\bm{L}_{\text{e}}
is the same in the Lindblad and quasi-Lindblad case, then the results for
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
in the above proof still holds (
Eqs.

C.10
and
C.11
), i.e.,
the only nonzero components of
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{O},\bm{O}^{\prime}}(t)
are

\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)=-\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{G}^{>}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)=\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}(t)=(\mathcal{G}^{>}(t))^{*},

-\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}^{++}(t)=\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)=\mathcal{G}^{<}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}(t)=\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}(t)=(\mathcal{G}^{<}(t))^{*}.

Therefore,

C_{4i-3,4j-2}(t)=\left((-\mathrm{i}\nu^{-}-M^{-})\mathcal{C}^{--}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}(t)((-\mathrm{i}\nu^{-})^{\dagger}-(M^{-})^{\dagger})\right)_{ij}=-\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t),

\displaystyle C_{4i-3,4j-1}(t)

\displaystyle=\left((-\mathrm{i}\nu^{+}+M^{+})\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\bm{c},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)(\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\dagger}+(M^{+})^{\dagger})+2M^{+}\mathcal{C}^{++}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}(t)(\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\dagger}+(M^{+})^{\dagger})\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\left((\nu^{+}-\mathrm{i}M^{+})G^{>}(t)((\nu^{+})^{\dagger}-\mathrm{i}(M^{+})^{\dagger})\right)_{ij}=-\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t),

C_{4i-2,4j-3}(t)=(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{*}+(M^{+})^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}^{++}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\text{T}}+(M^{+})^{\text{T}})_{ij}=-(\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t))^{*},

\displaystyle C_{4i-2,4j}(t)

\displaystyle=\left((-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{*}-(M^{-})^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)(\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{\text{T}}-(M^{-})^{\text{T}})+2(M^{-})^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)(\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{\text{T}}-(M^{-})^{\text{T}})\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\left(((\nu^{-})^{*}+\mathrm{i}(M^{-})^{*})(G^{<}(t))^{*}((\nu^{-})^{\text{T}}+\mathrm{i}(M^{-})^{\text{T}})\right)_{ij}=(\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t))^{*},

\displaystyle C_{4i-1,4j-3}(t)

\displaystyle=\left((\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{*}+(M^{+})^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}^{++}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\text{T}}+(M^{+})^{\text{T}})-2(M^{+})^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c}^{\dagger},\bm{c}}^{++}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\text{T}}+(M^{+})^{\text{T}})\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\left((\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{*}-(M^{+})^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\bm{c}}^{++}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\text{T}}+(M^{+})^{\text{T}})\right)_{ij}=(\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t))^{*},

C_{4i-1,4j}(t)=\left((\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{*}-(M^{-})^{*})\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}},\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)(\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{\text{T}}-(M^{-})^{\text{T}})\right)_{ij}=-(\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t))^{*},

\displaystyle C_{4i,4j-2}(t)

\displaystyle=\left((\mathrm{i}\nu^{-}-M^{-})\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{\dagger}-(M^{-})^{\dagger})-2M^{-}\mathcal{C}_{\bm{c},\bm{c}^{\dagger}}^{--}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{\dagger}-(M^{-})^{\dagger})\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\left((-\mathrm{i}\nu^{-}-M^{-})G^{<}(t)(-\mathrm{i}(\nu^{-})^{\dagger}-(M^{-})^{\dagger})\right)_{ij}=-\Delta_{ij}^{>}(t),

and finally,

C_{4i,4j-1}(t)=\left((\mathrm{i}\nu^{+}+M^{+})\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\bm{c}}^{\dagger},\widetilde{\bm{c}}}^{++}(t)(\mathrm{i}(\nu^{+})^{\dagger}+(M^{+})^{\dagger})\right)_{ij}=-\Delta_{ij}^{<}(t).

∎
